Most women know the unspoken checklist before getting into a car. They consider their surroundings, decide whether to chat or pretend to be on a phone call, and send a quick text to a friend with their live location, just in case. Getting from one place to another is rarely just a matter of distance; it comes with a quiet, constant layer of preparation and mental energy.

At Waymo, we believe women should get that energy back.

Our new film looks at what happens when the stress is taken out of the journey. Waymo allows our riders to take a work call, sit in total silence, or play music as loud as they want. We’re offering a space entirely on our riders’ own terms.

Riders are sharing that this shift matters. Our data shows that over half of women riders across established service markets reported that Waymo improves their sense of personal safety. Having a space where riders are in control of their environment makes a real difference.

To share this story, we were proud to partner with a women-led creative and production crew. The film was directed by Mollie Mills, with cinematography by Autumn Durald, and brought to life by executive producer Onye Anyanwu, editor Mah Ferraz, and music producer Shakari “Trakgirl” L.

How people move through the world is deeply personal. Women’s experiences and perspectives are an important part of how we think about the rider experience, including the value of having more choice, comfort and confidence when traveling.

Because having more choice in how we get around matters.

In Loving Memory of Tiger

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Catster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission at no cost to you. Learn more.

This article is part two of From Misfortune to Blessing: How a Cat Lost a Leg, But Got a Forever Home

Tiger came to my family on 2 June 2025 after an accident at a car park that resulted in his right hind leg being amputated. Before surgery, he was diagnosed with Stage B2 Hypertrophic Cardiomyopathy (HCM) â subclinical heart disease with a high risk of progression. We were advised to follow up on Tigerâs heart condition.

Having incurred a high bill for his condition in 2025, we brought Tiger to a general practice vet in Singapore, not a cardiologist. It was our first time rescuing a cat, and we didnât know the difference. The vet was more concerned about Tigerâs kidneys, and during several visits in 2025, she focused on his renal function. At each visit, she would listen to Tigerâs heart with a stethoscope and tell us the condition âshould still be the sameâ. Unknown to us, Tigerâs heart condition was quietly deteriorating.

In late December 2025, we suggested to the general vet that we book an echocardiogram to scan Tigerâs heart. We had to wait for the appointment, and it finally took place on 14 February 2026.

When Tiger came home from the echocardiogram, he was extremely weak. He had not eaten since midnight the previous day; the gabapentin used to relax him for the scan made him so weak that he could barely stand up, and he pooped on the floor.

The echocardiogram results took more than three weeks, and by then we were informed that Tigerâs heart condition had progressed from HCM stage B2 to congestive heart failure. Our vet asked for a one-minute video of Tigerâs breathing and told us his respiration rate was between 42 and 56, significantly higher than the normal upper limit of 30.

Two days later, Tiger had a thoracentesis procedure at our regular vet to remove excess fluid from around his lungs, and was discharged a few hours later. He was also placed on furosemide (a diuretic) and clopidogrel (a blood thinner).

It was our first time administering medicine to a cat. Despite our inexperience, we managed to administer furosemide as it was a liquid. Clopidogrel proved much harder to administer; we tried hiding the tablets inside a pill pocket, but Tiger hardly ate it.

In the initial few days after the thoracentesis procedure, Tigerâs breathing was less laboured. But I noticed that despite giving Tiger furosemide, he did not drink more water or urinate more often, and it didnât take long for fluid to accumulate in his lungs again.

On 25 March 2026, Tiger had his first major episode of congestive heart failure.

He groaned and lay on the floor; his breathing was laboured, and he was experiencing nystagmus (involuntary rhythmic movement of his eyeballs). We immediately took him to the emergency room at Beecroft Hospital.

At Beecroft Hospital, we were told that Tiger was about to âfall off the cliffâ and almost had a stroke. He had another thoracentesis procedure to remove fluid from his lungs and was placed in an oxygen chamber for observation for the next few days. We were told that the dosage prescribed by our regular vet was insufficient to control Tigerâs heart condition.

After a few days, Tiger was discharged on the maximum dose of furosemide, together with a combination of other medications (spironolactone, pimobendan, rivaroxaban, and a potassium supplement).

The hospital nurse demonstrated the proper ways to administer Tigerâs medicine, which our regular vet had not done. Initially, we dissolved the pills in a small amount of water and syringe-fed him, but this method was messy and wasted some of the medicine. Later, we placed the different medications in a single capsule and used a pill popper to administer them. This proved much more effective.

We had to feed the medicine at the same time each day, as delaying the diuretic by a few hours could cause the fluids to build up again. We had to adjust our life schedule around Tigerâs medication. We were also introduced to the Cardalis app to monitor Tigerâs resting respiratory rate.

After being discharged from the hospital, Tigerâs breathing was normal at first, but within a week, we were back in the emergency room. This time his diuretic was changed to torsemide, a stronger diuretic.

All in all, Tiger was in and out of the emergency room every week for a total of four visits, and the bills were mounting fast.

The cardiologist told us Tigerâs prognosis was poor and suggested euthanasia. We consulted an animal communicator who told us Tiger wanted to fight on. We were prepared for palliative care. February to April was a sad and emotionally draining period for us all.

With the stronger diuretic, his breathing rate was normal from May to July. We also gradually reintroduced his renal diet. It was a happy three months for us with many fond memories. He even bonded with my other cat during this time. For a while, we were able to forget about his palliative condition.

In early August, his breathing became laboured again, and he refused to eat his renal food, and I knew his kidneys would deteriorate fast on a normal diet. In the next two weeks, his appetite dropped, he hid more frequently around the house, and became increasingly lethargic.

From 20 August 2026 (Thursday) onwards, he would barely manage more than a few licks a day, and his weight dropped a lot. We could feel his spine and rib cage! He had not urinated for more than 24 hours, and we were so worried that his kidneys had totally shut down.

On Friday, miraculously, he urinated a few times and had some licks of food. His last meal was on Saturday night.

Tiger passed away naturally and peacefully at home on Sunday, 23 August 2026. He was so weak and lethargic, with barely any energy left, but still managed to get inside his little box and slept there. In this final moment, my sister calmed him to his final breath, and we were fortunate to witness his final, peaceful passing.

Throughout this ordeal, Tiger was very, very resilient and wanted to live, but he did not get the chance.

We held a private cremation for him on 24 August 2026, complete with Taoist chanting to guide him on his way. His ashes are stored at Mandai Pet Sanctuary in Singapore.

Despite the financial, emotional, and time sacrifices, it was an honour to take care of Tiger in the final stage of his life.

Now the room feels so empty in his absence.

Sheryl Sin is an editor, proofreader, indexer and writer based in Singapore. Outside her work, she is a committed community cat feeder who takes care of the street cats in her neighbourhood. She is also mum to Cow Girl (an FIV+ black-and-white domestic shorthair adopted off the street) and Tiger (the tripod cat with kidney and heart issues). Follow their journey on Instagram @sinmp24

Aina’s smart ring has a screen, a joystick, and a mic so you can talk to AI agents

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Hardware startup Aina released a smart ring today that features a screen, a joystick, and a mic so that you can talk to your agents and take notes. The $399 ring has a speaker so you can take calls using it as well.

The startup, which raised $5.5 million in July, is positioning the ring as a controller of sorts for both AI and non-AI apps.

The company previously made Mirage, a three-key controller that let you invoke buttons based on the app on your Mac’s screen. For instance, if you were on a Google Meet call, you could map keys to camera on/off, mic mute/unmute, or to leave the meeting.

Aina has baked in lessons from that device to create the new ring, which it’s simply calling “AI ring.” Using its mic, you can take notes, send a prompt to agents, set reminders, and dictate notes on your Mac using Wispr Flow or the startup’s own dictation engine.

Now, there are already smart rings in the market that do similar things, like the Pebble Index 01, Sandbar Stream, Oasis, and Natura, which is probably why Aina has added more controls and functionality to stand out. Its ring comes with a speaker so that you can take calls, and there is a screen, which lets you see notifications from your phone or which apps you are controlling.

For navigation, there is a joystick, and it also supports gestures, so that it can trigger different actions based on what’s on your Mac or phone’s screen. The ring also lets you control music or even smart home devices like Apple TV.

I have used the ring for a few days, and it feels like a smartwatch in a ring format, with more controls. I can get notifications and reply to them. It surfaces my meetings; I can call Uber if I want to get a ride home.

It’s easier to use Apple TV’s remote itself, but the Aina ring proved useful the couple times I couldn’t find the remote. Granted, a lot of the software and features are still being fleshed out, so it’s hard to figure out where such a device ring might sit in my daily usage, but it is squarely aimed at folks who are surrounded by digital devices and AI applications.

“Phones and laptop screens were designed for an age of browsing where you think about opening apps. For interacting with proactive agents, we need a better interface, and that is what we had in mind while designing Aina,” Apoorv Shankar, Aina’s founder, told TechCrunch over a call.

Companies are trying to cater to programmers and AI enthusiasts with specialized hardware that could help them harness different agents. This includes OpenAI’s experimental keyboard and Rabbit trying to position itself as a controller. The hope with such hardware is that enough people will have multiple agents running to invest in specific gadgets to keep tabs on them.

Aina’s ring goes on preorder starting Thursday at early-bird pricing of $399, and the company plans to start shipping the device in January 2027. Once the early-bird period ends, the ring will cost $499.

Se pensant hors antenne, Bertrand Latour révèle avoir gagné de l'argent en pariant sur les 10 buts inscrits du match Lens - Côme !

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Pensant être hors antenne après la rencontre rocambolesque entre le RC Lens et Côme, le journaliste de Canal+ Bertrand Latour s'est retrouvé piégé par un micro resté ouvert. Une confidence inattendue sur ses gains aux paris sportifs qui n'a pas manqué de faire réagir sur les réseaux sociaux.

Ce mercredi 4 novembre 2026, à l'occasion de la 4e journée de la phase de ligue de la Ligue des champions, le RC Lens a sombré sur sa pelouse du stade Bollaert-Delelis face aux Italiens de Côme (1-9). Une déroute sans précédent dans la prestigieuse compétition européenne, marquée par un scénario fou qui a manifestement profité à certains observateurs.

La séquence s'est produite lors de la reprise d'antenne sur le plateau de l'émission « Canal Champions Club », présentée par Hervé Mathoux sur Canal+. Alors que le programme reprenait son cours, les micros ouverts ont capté les échanges privés de Bertrand Latour avec les consultants présents à ses côtés, à savoir Olivier Dacourt, Laure Boulleau et Walid Regragui.

Toutefois, cette scène n'a pas été diffusée sur les antennes françaises de la chaîne cryptée. C'est en effet sur les flux internationaux retransmis hors de France que les téléspectateurs ont pu entendre les propos du journaliste en direct, avant que l'extrait ne soit largement relayé sur les réseaux sociaux. Un couac technique.

Se croyant à l'abri des antennes, Bertrand Latour a révélé avoir tenté un coup particulièrement audacieux au cours de cette soirée européenne. Le journaliste a ainsi précisé à ses collègues avoir misé la somme de 10 euros sur « 9 et 10 buts » dans la rencontre opposant les Lensois aux Transalpins.

Une inspiration payante au terme d'un scénario surréaliste. Grâce à cette avalanche de buts, Bertrand Latour a confié avoir remporté un gain de plus de 500 euros. De quoi provoquer l'étonnement de ses interlocuteurs sur le plateau, surpris par cette confidence.

En revanche, la soirée a été bien plus compliquée pour le RC Lens. En s'inclinant sur ce score fleuve de 9 à 1 face à Côme, le club artésien a en effet concédé la plus lourde défaite à domicile de l'histoire de la Ligue des champions. Ce revers historique relègue désormais la formation nordiste à la 33e place du classement de la phase de ligue.

Il faudra rapidement réagir pour chasser les doutes. Le prochain rendez-vous du RC Lens est fixé au dimanche 8 novembre 2026 à 20h45 au stade Bollaert-Delelis face à l'Olympique de Marseille, pour le compte de la 10e journée de Ligue 1.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/oscar-health-boasts-strongest-year-its-history-after-outperforming-q3" hreflang="en">Oscar Health boasts ‘strongest year’ in its history after outperforming in Q3</a>

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →
Oscar Health boasts ‘strongest year’ in its history after outperforming in Q3" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Oscar Health is seeing the “strongest year” in its history in 2026, CEO Mark Bertolini told investors during the company’s third quarter earnings call Thursday morning.

“We delivered record profitability for the first nine months of the year, reporting earnings from operations of $1.3 billion and net income of $1.2 billion,” he said. “We also expect to achieve several of our 2024 Investor Day commitments one year ahead of plan.”

Oscar Health brought in $5.1 billion in Q3 revenue, up “roughly 70%” year-over-year, Bertolini said. It beat analysts’ estimates of $4.72 billion.

Its medical loss ratio was 84%, down from 88.5% year-over-year, according to financial results published Thursday morning.

The company ended Q3 with 2.7 million members, up 27% year-over-year, Bertolini said, adding it is entering open enrollment with a “24% larger footprint” with plans to expand into South Carolina. “Our expansion increases our total addressable market to approximately 10 million consumers across 21 states,” he said.

Moreover, Oscar Health is launching a “new wave” of consumer lifestyle products and “addressing affordability through bundled benefits,” Bertolini said.

He also pointed to new artificial intelligence capabilities across the Oscar Health platform, including Lucie. Unveiled in April, the platform is a one-stop shop for individuals seeking health plans and supplemental benefit options.

“No other platform brings these capabilities together in one place, he said. “Lucie is on track to sell approximately 800,000 new policies in 2027, driven by growing broker and consumer demand.”

Based on the company’s performance, CFO Scott Blackley said Oscar Health is raising its 2026 full year outlook. Earnings are expected to be in a range of $850 million to $950 million, Blackley said.

Total revenue is expected to be in the range of $19 billion to $19.3 billion, which Blackley notes is “an increase of $300 million” from its prior outlook.

Blackley continued that the company expects to end the year with 2.5 million members, a figure that reflects “additional disenrollments from the second CMS review.” A full-year MLR of 80.5% to 81.5% is also expected.

De Roon: “I never had any doubts about joining Roma.”

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Marten De Roon gave an interview to Idealista.it in which he discussed his arrival in Rome.

“When the coach called me, I already knew it would be a difficult move, partly because of the history of Roma and Atalanta, partly because of my history with Roma on social media; but at a certain point we had made up our minds, I had no doubts. The pitch speaks for itself. I had no doubts; I wanted to prove on the pitch my desire to be here. Gasperini is also demonstrating this; facts count, there’s time.”

The Dutch midfielder explained how he experiences football: “In two different ways. On the pitch, I want to win at all costs. My main quality, what I like to do, is to see the game. I’m tactically prepared, it’s my strength: I see where we can attack well, where we can defend better. From midfield, I like to manage the team, call the markers. Off the pitch, I like to joke, talk, and, above all, observe. I like trying to help my teammates; I’m a professional, and even off the pitch, I try to be helpful, given my age and experience.”

The former Atalanta player spoke about the Giallorossi dressing room: “There are a lot of nice people. I’m still finding my role, so I don’t joke around too much, but the atmosphere is healthy; it seems like an ‘easy’ dressing room. Years ago, I thought it was a very difficult dressing room, even playing against them; now I’ve come in and I’ve seen that it’s healthy, warm, and welcoming. I already knew some of them, obviously Malen, Rensch, Mancini, Gollini, and Cristante. This obviously helps; it’s made things easier.”

De Roon gave a behind-the-scenes look at how he looked for a place to live in the capital: “I spoke to the guys here in the locker room, Pellegrini helped me get to know the city, he gave me a hand with so many things, together with the club.”

Marten De Roon spoke about his relationship with Gasperini: “We spent eight years together, we had the best time with Atalanta, we fought so many battles together, and I’ve always thanked him. Our relationship is above all professional; if he’s your coach, you don’t see him off the pitch during the week. Last year, he called me every now and then, asking about Malen, but more than anything he wanted confirmation; he was already convinced. I had no doubts. He’s doing pretty well, right?”

Did the backlash make Nvidia nerf the AI tech?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

As if Potterheads didn't have enough to worry about amid the controversy around the design changes in HBO's Harry Potter series, Hogwarts Legacy now has Nvidia DLSS 5 enhancements. The next generation of Nvidia's AI-driven neural rendering and upscaling came to the game today, and those following the backlash over the tech were prepared for the worst.

So does DLSS 5 ride roughshod over Hogwarts Legacy's art direction and create entirely new characters like people expected when the leaked unfinished DLSS 5 iteration surfaced online back in August? Erm...

As demonstrated by Nvidia in the launch trailer above, the impact of DLSS 5 in Hogwarts Legacy looks surprisingly subtle. The chip giant maintains that the aim is to enhance lighting and material quality to "boost environmental uplift while preserving character identity".

"With the addition of DLSS 3D-Guided Neural Rendering to Hogwarts Legacy, our complete suite of DLSS technologies will now be supported, enabling you to accelerate and enhance your experiences in the wizarding world," Nvidia says.

It seems it just can't please people. After the ridicule Nvidia faced for the initial tests of DLSS 5, some people are now saying that the actual implementation in Hogwarts Legacy is laughably subtle. Some are struggling to see a worthwhile difference even in side-by-side comparisons "Before vs Before", one person jokes in the comments on the trailer above.

What do you think of the comparisons below?

"People complained too much that they just completely nerfed it. Now we just have fps loss with basically little difference in image quality," one person complains in the comments on YouTube.

"I find it hilarious how the first presentation was so completely over the top strength wise, and now it's reduced to a point where you really can't even tell if it's even on anymore without a direct comparison," someone else writes.

"Asking for another 100-200 watts of power for basically no difference is crazy work," a third person suggests.

Alas, the relatively subtle implementation is unlikely to end the DLSS 5 controversy. While some scenes arguably benefit from a subtle boost in contrast, the extra detail in some of the environments actually makes them feel less atmospheric in my opinion.

Official DLSS 5 support is currently limited to GeForce RTX 50 Series GPUs and RTX Spark systems. Activating DLSS 3D-Guided Neural Rendering, 6X Multi Frame Generation, Ray Reconstruction, NVIDIA Reflex, and ray tracing, allows Hogwarts Legacy to run at a max of 4K at up to 300 frames per second on RTX 5090 (see our guide the best GPUs).

"For an optimal experience, we also recommend enabling DLSS Super Resolution under 'Upscale Type', DLSS Multi Frame Generation under 'Frame Generation', NVIDIA Reflex under 'Low Latency Mode', and 'DLSS Ray Reconstruction' if you're also activating Hogwarts Legacy's immersive ray-traced effects," Nvidia adds.

DLSS 5 is also coming to the free-to-play military combat game War Thunder from mid-November and to the wuxia-inspired Where Winds Meet from 23 November. You can read the full blog post on the Nvidia website

Nvidia said back in March that its next-generation DLSS would bring "photorealism" to gaming through the use of a new generative AI model designed to enhance frames and add detail to lighting, textures and materials in real time. But the GPU maker's early demo immediately sparked controversy by giving Grace from Capcom’s Resident Evil Requiem a glamour makeover.

Leaked through the nvngx_dlssnr.dll library via NBA 2K27, modders soon began testing DLSS 5 for themselves and sharing the videos and screenshots on social media. Faces were the main area of contention, with some modders complaining that Nvidia's new tech makes characters look uncanny and AI-generated or older than they're supposed to be by sharpening skin texture.

That sparked a big debate around artistic intent, with some people concerned that the technology would effectively change game artists' original vision.

Hyperliquid (HYPE) Whales Turn Stan Druckenmiller's Hype Into a $5 Million Exit

According to a fresh investigation by Santiment, retail investors, giving in to "fear of missing out" on Hyperliquid (HYPE) price rally on October 30, gave four large wallets a perfect opportunity to fully close their positions, bagging $5 million in profit.

Wall Street legend Stan Druckenmiller's first ever appearance on X (former Twitter) with a post about Hyperliquid and the tokenization of trading was supposed to be a declaration of a new era for the popular DeFi exchange. It turned out to be a "sell the news" signal for HYPE tokens' whales.

The best example of 'cashing out' math was educated by the address "0xa2ce...3126". What was a constant accumulation since the end of May, at the peak of the pump, in the $94–$95 range for HYPE token, became a methodical dump worth exactly $225,000 on the market every five minutes. All this was happening while the crowd bought up on the news. The result is a completely empty wallet and its owner locking in $2,395,168 gains.

This whale was not alone in his approach and as Santiment stresses, citing its own on-chain data, four top players opened short positions simultaneously and are now in profit. The only big buyer who believed in HYPE price growth is losing more than 11%.

The token's price chart against USD only confirms a total exodus of big buyers at the time of writing. The Oct. 30 pump candle left a huge upper wick with a trace of those very five-minute sales. The HYPE price has already slid more than 12% and is squeezed in the $81.79–$85 range. The local level of $84.150 has been broken from top to bottom, and now the token is slowly drifting to the next strong support at $78.020.

Traditional finance's 'big guns' like Druckenmiller undoubtedly remain the best marketing tool for crypto projects. The only difference is that for retail investors a billionaire’s tweet became a call to action, and for big capital an exit order.

The buyers’ rush created a perfect liquidity window for the whales — a chance to sell off huge volumes of coins without the price slipping down. What this makes of the story is that the technology and business of Hyperliquid turned out not to equal the price of decentralized exchange's native token.

Court to consider the reach of Article III courts in immigration contract dispute

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Type to search articles, cases, and authors.

The argument on Nov. 10 in Department of Labor v. Sun Valley Orchards brings the justices a complex constitutional problem sure to get their focused attention: does the Constitution allow Congress to delegate to the executive branch – as opposed to the courts – the authority to adjudicate the obligation of an employer to comply with employment agreements it makes with temporary immigrant workers.

Here, the Department of Labor, employing administrative law judges, for several decades has adjudicated disputes under the H-2A visa program, which allows employers to hire temporary foreign agricultural workers. The Department of Labor argues that Sun Valley Orchards failed in several ways to compensate such workers as it promised the government it would to qualify for the H-2A program. As is typical in such cases, administrative employees of the DOL (in its wage and hour division) charged Sun Valley with failing to live up to its obligations, and the DOL for the most part persuaded an ALJ that it was correct. On appeal, a different group of the DOL’s employees (its administrative review board) accepted the ALJ’s findings. At that point, for the first time, Sun Valley had a chance to take the dispute before Article III judges, but under the applicable statute the federal district court owes great deference to the DOL’s adjudication.

The case comes to the justices because the U.S. Court of Appeals for the 3rd Circuit reasoned that the administrative adjudication of the department’s charges cannot be reconciled with Article III of the Constitution.

At a high level of generality, the doctrinal framework here is quite clear. All agree that Congress constitutionally can authorize adjudication by executive branch officials rather than judges, and all agree that its power to do so is limited to cases that involve “public rights,” a notoriously vague category designed to distinguish traditionally executive actions (like granting a visa) from disputes traditionally resolved in court (like tort and contract litigation). When those adjudications go beyond public rights, though, they infringe on the judiciary’s exclusive authority under Article III. The difficulty is that when you get down to details – defining the boundaries of “public rights” – the rulings have neither been stable nor persuasive. The point was made best by Chief Justice William Rehnquist (channeling Matthew Arnold), who famously characterized the decisions as “landmarks on a judicial ‘darkling plain’ where ignorant armies have clashed by night.”

For this particular dispute, the government contends that the case plainly involves public rights because it involves immigration or because it involves the assessment of remedies for transgression of privileges granted by the executive. By contrast, the employer characterizes the subject of the adjudication as a contract dispute. Granting the government’s free ability to decide (and adjudicate if it wishes to do so) the entitlement of any particular immigrants or group of immigrants to a visa or other immigration status, the dispute here involves whether the employer has complied with its employment contract. Because that is a prototypically private dispute – the kind of thing that could have been brought before a court at Westminster in Old England in the days of the drafting of Article III – the employer contends that it is not the kind of public right that Congress can have adjudicated by the executive.

It would be naïve to expect the justices will feel themselves constrained by the existing precedents on this topic. The case directly implicates the prerogatives of the judicial branch and the justices will see it that way. Moreover, decisions like Loper Bright Enterprises v. Raimondo and Trump v. Slaughter show a bench with a great appetite for reining in the administrative state even when precedents are settled and directly on point. And there is another problem for the government here – many of the cases they rely on predate the days of FDR and the rise of the administrative state. Furthermore, none of them crisply validates an administrative adjudication that takes money from one private party and gives it to another.

I would expect Justice Neil Gorsuch to be a major figure at the argument, as he has been pressing this particular point for several years, albeit in the context of the securities laws. For him, the government’s contention that the lower court decision would disrupt a large swath of long-accepted administrative arrangements will be a feature, not a bug. Chief Justice John Roberts also can be expected to be an important player, as he has written several of the modern opinions in the area. So this seemingly narrow dispute may herald something larger: yet another blow against the administrative state.

Ronald Mann is a professor of law at Columbia, where he teaches courses in commercial finance, payment systems and deals.

West Ham Owners’ ‘Marriage Of Convenience’ Could End With Staveley Takeover

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

West Ham’s ownership saga has taken another interesting turn following comments from former football executive Christian Purslow, who believes Daniel Křetínský and David Sullivan could eventually sell the club to Amanda Staveley once the Hammers return to the Premier League.

Speaking to football journalist Henry Winter on The Football Boardroom podcast, Purslow described the relationship between West Ham’s two principal shareholders as a “marriage of convenience” and questioned why the previously announced agreement to purchase Vanessa Gold’s shares has still not been formally completed.

Regular readers of Claret & Hugh, and indeed viewers of Hammers Chat, will not be particularly surprised by any of this. I’ve been describing Sullivan and Křetínský as business partners for some time, and the possibility of Staveley eventually returning to the negotiating table is something we’ve discussed at length.

Purslow’s comments came during a fascinating discussion with Henry Winter about West Ham’s ownership situation, prompted by the dismissal of Nuno Espírito Santo and the appointment of Scott Parker.

The former Liverpool and Aston Villa chief executive suggested that Sullivan and Křetínský may have very little in common, but they share one overriding ambition: getting West Ham back into the Premier League as quickly as possible.

That, according to Purslow, is enough to keep their working relationship together, even if their longer-term ambitions for the football club are different.

And frankly, I think he’s absolutely right.

Ever since the agreement was reached for Křetínský to purchase the bulk of Vanessa Gold’s shares, with Sullivan increasing his own holding from approximately 38.8% to 40%, I’ve referred to the pair as business partners. Indeed, Claret & Hugh reported last month that a club insider had specifically described their relationship as a “joint partnership”.

I appreciate that this might be uncomfortable for some West Ham supporters to hear, particularly those who hoped Sullivan’s departure from his executive position would signal the beginning of the end of his involvement.

But let’s be fair, a man who wants less financial involvement in a football club doesn’t ordinarily increase his shareholding. Whatever anybody thinks of Sullivan, the proposed arrangement would leave him owning approximately 40% of West Ham, and that’s hardly the behaviour of somebody desperate to walk away immediately.

Staveley has been locked out of West Ham for now

What I found particularly interesting was Purslow questioning something we’ve also discussed repeatedly on this website. Why has the agreement to increase Křetínský’s shareholding to 46% still not been formally completed?

The arrangement was announced back in September, with the existing shareholders exercising their rights to purchase Vanessa Gold’s stake and subsequently agreeing to redistribute shares. As our original investigation into the transaction explained, the process was designed to leave Křetínský as West Ham’s largest shareholder, Sullivan on approximately 40% and Tripp Smith on around 11%.

Yet here we are in November, and the anticipated confirmation of the completed transaction has still not arrived. Purslow admitted he found that puzzling and wondered whether the agreement might have encountered difficulties, although he was careful to acknowledge that he did not know whether that was the case.

I completely agree with him that the delay is odd, although I wouldn’t go so far as to suggest the agreement has collapsed. In fact, when Claret & Hugh last enquired, we were assured that there were no problems with the transaction and that completion was expected soon.

Nevertheless, there is another important point here. Křetínský indicated that he would engage with supporters once his increased shareholding had been finalised, and we’re still waiting for that conversation.

Given the importance of the proposed ownership changes, I don’t think it’s unreasonable for supporters to expect some clarity about when the transaction will be completed and what the future structure of the club will look like.

Kretinsky successfully blocked Vanessa Gold’s attempts to sell her shareholding to Staveley but has yet to purchase her shares according to Purslow

Perhaps the most fascinating part of Purslow’s analysis concerned Amanda Staveley, who previously attempted to purchase the Gold family’s stake before the existing shareholders moved to prevent her from acquiring it.

Purslow suggested that once West Ham have secured promotion to the Premier League, both Sullivan and Křetínský could decide that the time is right to sell the entire football club. He also identified Staveley as a possible buyer, pointing to her previous interest, the work she has already undertaken examining the club and her ambitions for the London Stadium.

His assessment was particularly interesting:

“I have a feeling Amanda Staveley might well reappear because she would be the natural buyer for the whole club.”

Now, this is obviously Purslow’s prediction rather than confirmation of any agreement between the shareholders and Staveley. But I have to say, it’s a scenario I’ve considered entirely plausible for some time.

I’ve never fully believed that Staveley had gone away completely. For somebody who appeared so determined to purchase a football club, there has been remarkably little public indication of her pursuing another opportunity since her attempt to buy into West Ham was blocked.

Of course, that doesn’t mean she hasn’t been exploring other possibilities privately. But we already know she has shown a serious interest in West Ham, and Claret & Hugh previously revealed that discussions had taken place over the possibility of her acquiring Sullivan’s shares if the Gold family route proved unsuccessful.

Purslow also raised the question of valuations. He suggested Staveley’s previous proposal valued West Ham at around £600million, while Sullivan has historically placed a considerably higher value on the club. Promotion would clearly change the financial picture, making a potential sale more attractive than it would be while West Ham remain in the Championship.

The suggestion that Sullivan and Křetínský are effectively working together until West Ham secure promotion makes a certain amount of sense to me. Both have an enormous financial interest in the club returning to the Premier League, and that shared objective could be enough to maintain their partnership regardless of any differences between them.

It also puts the appointment of Scott Parker into an interesting context. Having backed Nuno to lead the promotion campaign during the summer, the shareholders have now agreed to change direction and appoint a manager with three Championship promotions on his CV.

Purslow clearly believes promotion could become the catalyst for something much bigger than another managerial change. His suggestion is that the two principal shareholders might eventually decide to cash in, potentially creating the opportunity for Staveley to return with a proposal to purchase the whole club rather than simply a minority stake.

Whether that happens remains to be seen, and there is certainly no evidence of an agreed sale. But I find it difficult to dismiss the possibility, particularly given everything we’ve already been told about the relationship between Sullivan and Křetínský and Staveley’s previous determination to get involved.

For me, the most revealing aspect of Purslow’s comments is that he has independently arrived at a number of the same questions we’ve been asking for months. Why is the share transaction taking so long? What exactly is the relationship between Sullivan and Křetínský? And has Amanda Staveley really disappeared from the picture?

I suspect we haven’t heard the last of any of those three questions.

Blackburn Rovers sweat over injuries to two key defenders as West Ham challenge awaits

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Blackburn Rovers face West Ham in their final game before the November international break this Saturday. They come into the tie off the back of a 2-1 defeat against Stoke City, and Tony Mowbray’s side were without centre-back Tom Atcheson for the tie, while full-back Ryan Alebiosu came off in the first half.

Now, speaking to the press, Mowbray has said the Rovers duo will require further assessment to see if they can face the Hammers as they struggle with minor issues. As quoted by the club’s official media, the boss said: “Big Tom has still got a little niggle in his groin, so it’s about whether we take a chance with him or not. Ryan is going to go for a scan, and I hope it isn’t too bad.”

Alebiosu has been a mainstay for Blackburn Rovers this season, starting every Championship match and playing the full 90 minutes in all but two games. Atcheson meanwhile has made 11 appearances in all competitions, including 10 league starts.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t blessed for depth, so it would be a blow to go into Saturday’s game against West Ham without Alebiosu and Atcheson. Mowbray will have to work out a solution if either are ruled out.

Alebiosu has been the mainstay at right-back this season, but his absence would likely offer an opportunity for summer signing Gady Beyuku to impress. The 20-year-old has only made four substitute appearances to date, but he came on for 49 minutes against the Potters on Tuesday.

At centre-back, Hayden Carter partnered Sean McLoughlin with Atcheson out. The former has had injury problems of his own in recent times, so Mowbray may be hesitant to put him in for another full 90 minutes just a few days later.

This is an area where Mowbray has very little depth though. Scott Wharton was the only other central defender in the squad on Tuesday, and he has been on a long road back of his own.

It means Carter may start again if Atcheson is out, with Wharton called upon off the bench late on.

Of course, the hope will be that both Alebiosu and Atcheson are fit and ready to go against West Ham this weekend. However, if they aren’t deemed fit, the Blackburn Rovers defenders will have the international break to recover and rest up before the next club fixture.

After Saturday, the next league fixture sees Rovers head to Turf Moor to face rivals Burnley. Mowbray will be determined to have key men Alebiosu and Atcheson available for the fixture.

One potential worry is Atcheson’s call-up to the Northern Ireland squad for the upcoming international fixtures. If he isn’t deemed fit for Saturday, there will be questions over whether he should link up with Michael O’Neill‘s national side as planned.

Nigeria have not announced their squad for their AFCON qualifiers, but Alebiosu would be in a similar predicament if he is included in their ranks.

Democratic sweep puts crypto on defensive in Washington

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Democrats' sweep of Congress likely resets the political calculus for crypto, ushering in a more adversarial approach in Washington and pushing industry-friendly legislation like the Clarity Act to the back burner.

With crypto critics like Sherrod Brown, D-Ohio, returning to the Senate and Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., retaining significant influence over financial policy, analysts say the new Congress is likely to focus on scrutinizing President Donald Trump's personal crypto dealings over supportive industry legislation.

For years, crypto fought an uphill battle in Washington, asking for clear operating rules, but often landing on the receiving end of regulation by enforcement. That shifted after Trump's 2024 election victory, giving the industry a reprieve and seat at the policy table.

"We see Sherrod Brown winning in Ohio as damaging the crypto industry's political clout as the industry worked to defeat him," Jaret Seiberg, TD Cowen's Washington analyst, said in a note Wednesday. "We did not think Clarity could advance over the next two years. This makes us more confident in our prediction."

The crypto industry spent more than $200 million to sway the midterms, hoping to get leaders elected that could push a comprehensive crypto market structure bill over the finish line after the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate.

Seiberg emphasized that Democrats "will investigate Trump's crypto entanglements including World Liberty Financial" and said "the resulting political environment may make it impossible to advance a version of Clarity that the crypto sector could back."

Mike Townsend, Charles Schwab's managing director of legislative and regulatory affairs, said he expects "Democrats to push next year for a version of the bill that is much tougher on cryptocurrency companies."

The stakes extend beyond market structure legislation via Clarity. Stablecoin innovation; decentralized finance, or DeFi, development; banking access; tokenization; and sensible tax policy are priorities the industry sees as important to ensuring the U.S. remains competitive in the global crypto economy.

The industry will need continued bipartisan engagement to ensure these priorities aren't sidelined by changing political dynamics. And Democrats have historically been less supportive of crypto than Republicans, though that divide has narrowed as more Democrats embrace the industry.

The implications of Democrats' big win aren't all bad. Crypto has reached a level of financial integration that could be difficult to reverse, with its future increasingly centered on establishing crypto as financial infrastructure. Even with the Clarity Act potentially stalled until 2028, key financial regulators can help advance the industry through rule-making.

Coinbase's vice president of U.S. policy, Kara Calvert, points out that the election results point to a historic shift in the makeup of the incoming Congress, with a record number of pro-crypto voices expected to join its ranks.

"With last night's results, the 120th Congress is on track to have more new pro-crypto members than we've ever seen in any freshman class," she said. "Crypto is 100% bipartisan, and that's why the future of finance does not rest on either political party or a single election night. Democrats and Republicans alike know that clear rules protect consumers and keep innovation in the U.S."

As of Thursday morning, 19 candidates characterized as pro crypto by the advocacy nonprofit Stand With Crypto have been elected to the Senate versus eight Senate candidates it characterizes as anti crypto. In the House, 228 pro-crypto candidates have been elected versus 106 the group deems anti crypto.

Several races have not been called, but Fairshake — the super PAC launched in 2023 to support pro-crypto candidates regardless of party — has so far won 29 of the 34 races it spent on, per Reuters, including three of the six House candidates it prioritized by spending a baseline of $1 million on their campaigns.

Dante Disparte, chief strategy officer and head of global policy and operations at Circle, emphasized Circle's bipartisan approach and readiness for new Senate and House leadership, but said it "may take longer to get Clarity tabled again."

That bipartisan support may offer a blueprint for what comes next, but even if crypto legislation stalls, progress can continue, said Joseph Chalom, CEO of ether treasury company Sharplink.

"We expect the Clarity Act to remain on hold through 2028. Even so, there is a clear path forward for stablecoins … and for the tokenization of public equities under the SEC's five-year innovation exemption," he said. "Technological progress is rarely rolled back. Once the benefits of faster settlement, lower costs and markets that never close become clear to investors and institutions, this shift will be very difficult to reverse, regardless of who wins the White House in 2028."

Near-term, investors should expect more volatility as the market tries to figure out how crypto regulation may shift under a Democratic Congress.

The impact of the blue wave "should vary considerably across assets," said James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares. "We would expect U.S. crypto equities to be more sensitive than bitcoin, particularly businesses reliant on new regulatory permissions. Established, compliant operators could even benefit from greater scrutiny of competitors. So miners who already have data centers set up are likely to remain in high demand by AI hyperscalers."

— CNBC's Talia Kaplan contributed reporting.

Tether CEO Reacts to Massive USDT Demand in Paraguay

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino has responded to claims that the company's stablecoin, USDT, is being increasingly sought after in Paraguay following a significant banking crisis that cut off millions of people from their savings. Ardinoino has described this as "a flight to safety."

As noted in a recent X post by Bitcoin proponent Samson Mow, Tether's USDT has seen a significant rise in value relative to the Paraguayan guaraní amid the collapse of Ueno Bank, one of Paraguay's biggest digital lenders.

Ueno Bank, with an estimated 3 million customers (a significant percentage of adult Paraguayans), has been hit by a major financial crisis.

According to Mow, USDT has risen from 5,692 guaraníes to over 6,000 guaraníes, an increase of over 5%.

The surge in interest in the U.S. dollar-backed stablecoin USDT, Mow argues, is due to people trying to safeguard their savings outside of the traditional banking system.

USDT's exchange rate rose from about 5,692 guaraníes on Oct. 15 to over 6,000 on Nov. 4.

It is worth noting that even the traditional U.S. dollar rose against the guaraní over the same period.

The rising interest in USDT comes as Paraguay faces a major financial crisis over the collapse of Ueno Bank.

On Nov. 3, Paraguay's central bank announced the start of resolution proceedings for the financial institution after discovering that Ueno Bank's solvency ratio had dropped below half the minimum limit.

The move essentially shut down Ueno Bank, cutting off millions of customers' access to their savings and standard banking services for the time being.

The crisis has also cast doubt on public funds parked at Ueno Bank, including hundreds of millions of dollars from Paraguay's social security system.

The scandal has reportedly already led to the arrest of eight people connected to the bank as well as the resignation of central bank president Carlos Carvallo, as reported by Reuters.

Paraguay's central bank has been trying to reassure depositors, stressing that the situation pertains specifically to Ueno Bank and is not indicative of the collapse of the country's banking system.

Customers with frozen funds at Ueno Bank cannot necessarily convert their deposits to stablecoins until they are able to access their money again.

How to Address Common Causes of Knee Pain While Cycling

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Identify common reasons your knees may hurt while riding, and what to do about it.

As a cyclist, you probably know that you should always listen to your knees and the messages they send when they’re not so happy. These messages show up as aches and pains, and they’re common among those who ride. While research suggests cycling can actually help you avoid knee pain later in life, other studies indicate it still affects about 15 to 30 percent of cyclists.

Fortunately, the culprit is often an easy fix, but the first step is to identify what may be causing your knee pain. For example, you may be overtraining and pushing your body harder than it’s used to, straining your connective tissues and causing inflammation and pain.

Any spontaneous twinges of crank-stopping knee pain may feel like they come out of no where, when actually they’re just the first noticeable symptoms of a long-brewing problem. This generally stems from equipment and/or bike position issues, says bike-fit specialist Michael Veal of BikeDynamics in Warwickshire, England.

“Many cyclists are forever tinkering with their position, looking for that elusive perfect position,” he says. But what feels just right for a few pedal strokes around the block can end up feeling pretty wrong after 70 miles of roads. A good bike fit is essential for preventing most causes of knee pain.

For more guidance on what your knee pain means and strategies to avoid it, read on.

WANT MORE INFO ON BIKE FIT? CHECK OUT OUR EXCLUSIVE PROGRAM!

To help you figure out where to start, here are the most common types of cycling knee pain and the ways in which your bike fit may contribute to each symptom.

If your knee hurts right on the cap (or patella), it’s typically because of your quads. The four quadricep muscles attach to the shin via the patella, and when you’re putting force into your pedal stroke, these muscles deliver that force across the knee joint.

Your bike set-up can help with this problem. Determine whether adjusting your saddle height, saddle fore and aft, and/or crank length could alleviate the issue. Working with a professional bike fitter can be extremely helpful in addressing this type of knee pain.

“A saddle that is too low will keep your knee angle too tight at the top of the stroke, which increases the shear forces pulling the patella against the femur, which in turn increases the likelihood of tendonitis and harmful stresses in the cartilage behind the kneecap,” Veal says. “Likewise when the cranks are too long for your leg length, the knee joint is also too tight at the top of the stroke.”

How low is too low for a saddle? “For a quick reference to check if your saddle is the right height, have a seat and rest your heel on the pedal with the pedal in the 6 o’clock position,” says fit specialist and physical therapist Sara Bresnick, founder of Pedal Power Training Solutions in Medford, Massachusetts. “The knee of that leg should be straight. That equates to a 20- to 25-degree knee bend when clipped in.”

Riding with your saddle too far forward also can cause knee pain in the front, says Veal. “Your saddle should be positioned so the tibial tuberosity—or the bony bit below the kneecap—is directly above the ball of the foot, which in turn should be above the pedal spindle,” she says.

Also, be mindful of your form. Big-gear mashing, climbing in monster gears, and hard sprinting (especially if you’re not conditioned for it) can put undue stress on the knee and cause this type of pain.

If you do achieve a solid bike fit and avoid these common mistakes, and pain still persists, focus on stretching the quads to find relief.

Pain behind the knee is less common than pain in the front, and is generally easier to trace back to a culprit: over-extending the knee, says Bresnick. Your saddle is too high or too far back. “Try lowering the saddle a bit or moving it forward a bit in relation to the handlebars,” she says.

This pain is also more common among cyclists who spend a lot of time on fixed-gear bikes; When you ride fixed, you use your hamstrings to decelerate your pedal stroke, which can put the biceps femoris tendon (a hamstring muscle that runs down the back of your leg toward the outside of your knee) under too much load and irritate it. Giving your legs a break with some freewheeling now and then can also help.

If pain sticks around after making bike adjustments, try stretching the hamstrings regularly and avoid frequent out-of-saddle riding.

When you feel pain on the inner (or medial sides) of your knees, look down at your feet: Cleat placement is often a culprit, says Veal. Your cleat position affects your Q-factor, which dictates how far apart your feet are laterally when pedaling.

Ideally, the spacing should be that the loads from your knees to your pedals are traveling vertically without pushing the knee inward or outward, which stresses the ligaments on either side of your knee and can lead to pain.

Cleats positioned too close to the insides of your cycling shoes increase the distance between your feet, which can stress the inside collateral ligaments and cause pain in the inner (or medial) sides of your knees.

Too much float in your pedals also can cause medial knee pain. A little float—about 4.5 degrees—is all you need to be comfortable and keep your knees from getting stressed.

While a professional can help you adjust cleat positioning, you can also have someone take a slow-motion video you riding on a trainer, recording movement from the front of the bike. Look for asymmetry between your left and right side.

If you still feel pain on the inside of your knee after aligning your cleats, especially if it is a burning pain at rest or sharp pain when you start to walk or step up or down, you may have a type of bursitis. This pain typically improves with a warmup and stretching of the hip flexors and adductors (inner thighs).

Pain on the outer (or lateral) sides of the knees is often caused by iliotibial (IT) band syndrome—a stabbing pain that happens when the IT band (a fibrous connective tissue band that runs from the hip along the thigh to the tibia) becomes stressed and inflamed.

IT band issues can appear to come out of nowhere, and hurt most when the knee is at 20- to 30-degrees of flexion, like when you approach the 6 o’clock position and start to come back up.

“Misaligned cleats that cause the foot to be excessively toed in can cause this,” says Veal. So can cleats that are placed too far to the outsides of the shoes, causing too narrow of a stance on the bike.

If you think the cleats on your bike are misaligned, take your bike in for an adjustment. (A slow-mo video of you riding from the front can also help you see if your cleat positioning is to blame!)

Of course, what you do on your bike will also go a long way to promoting or alleviating knee pain. Here are some tips to assure it’s the latter.

Just as with most sports, a little warmup to get blood flowing and muscles primed is a good idea—so don’t skip it! As a general rule, aim for at least 15 minutes of moderate spinning before you attack the local climb or go hard on the flats.

Look at video of the pros in action, and you’ll notice their rapid and fluid pedal strokes. Try this drill to improve your pedal stroke: The next time you are going up your favorite climb, use a gear at least two teeth lower (thus easier) than usual. This higher cadence is easier on your joints. Think about making round circles as you pedal, not ovals.

Your first ride back after any extended amount of time off shouldn’t be five hours long. Instead, start with a lower mileage and build slowly.

Your body likes consistency, so when you alter equipment, your knees often protest. Whether you get a new bike, new shoes, new pedals, or change stem length, go easy at first to give yourself time to adapt.

Riding in 40-degree weather with red, chapped knees is a recipe for trouble. Pros routinely cover their legs in training when the temperature is below 60 degrees—that’s why knee warmers exist!

While each individual’s tolerance will differ, there’s certainly no harm in keeping muscles and joints warm. By using warmers, you can easily slip them off mid-ride if you start to warm up.

Take note of what gear works best in certain weather conditions, so you can repeat on future rides.

If knee pain persists, consider getting a professional bike fit. And no, we’re not talking about when a salesperson sets your seat height at purchase.

A professional bike fit should be an in-depth experience with a professionally trained fitter that includes an interview process, off-bike flexibility testing, and on-bike measurements and adjustments. Yes, it’s an investment (usually at least $200), but it’s worth it to ride pain-free. It could end up being the best money you ever spent on your cycling habit.

If you’re still experiencing cycling knee pain after doing everything mentioned above, including a professional bike fit, it may be caused by muscle imbalances instead—like weak glutes, hips, or core. It’s best to consult with a sports medicine physician and/or physical therapist to find the right course of treatment for you.

John Vasudevan, M.D. is an associate professor at the University of Pennsylvania. He is board-certified in Physical Medicine & Rehabilitation and Sports Medicine. He is a Team Physician for UPenn Athletics and  medical director of the Broad Street Run and Philadelphia Distance Run, and previously for the Rock 'n' Roll Half-Marathon and Tri-Rock Triathlon in Philadelphia. He is a director of the running and endurance Sports Medicine Program at Penn Medicine.  Dr. Vasudevan provides non-operative management of musculoskeletal conditions affecting athletes and active individuals of all levels, and combines injury rehabilitation with injury prevention. He utilizes a variety of ultrasound-guided procedures and regenerative approaches such as platelet-rich plasma and percutaneous ultrasonic tenotomy. He sees patients at the Penn Medicine and the Philadelphia Veterans Administration hospital. Cristiano Ronaldo’s 143 international goals: Could any of these five players surpass him?

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo has announced his international retirement and set a very high bar for the top goalscorer of all time.

After 234 caps, Ronaldo confirmed that he was retiring from the Portuguese national team and does so having scored 146 goals for the team in a 23-year stint.

That tally puts him top of the all-time scorers for their national team with the also-retired Lionel Messi next best with 20 fewer goals.

Ali Daei of Iran makes up the top three but are there any players who could beat Ronaldo’s record?

The current highest-ranked active player is Lukaku who has scored 94 goals for Belgium.

His age, however, makes it very difficult for him to reach Ronaldo’s tally. Lukaku is 33 now and scores at a rate of 0.7 per match, that means he needs another 76 matches to score 147 goals.

He plays between 5-10 international games per year and so would need to play for another seven years or so which would take him to the age Ronaldo is now.

It’s not impossible but is unlikely you would figure.

If you’re looking for a 33-year-old who could break the record then ignore Lukaku and instead focus on Kane.

The reason why Kane is a more tempting bet is he is in ridiculous goal scoring form, the kind of form that wins you the Ballon d’Or.

Kane’s got 91 in 125 England games, meaning he’s at 0.73 per match, his numbers though have really ramped up in the last five seasons.

He got 16 in 2021, including four in the delayed Euro 2020, and 13 in 2026 with more games yet to come.

If he continues at the rate of his career so far, he would need 77 more matches but he has a one-in-one record for 2026. Continue at that pace and he would reach the tally a lot quicker.

If you were a betting man, you would probably put your money on Mbappe to be the one to reach it.

He’s got a lot of good things going for him. First of all, he’s the main goalscoring output for a nation that regularly reaches major tournament finals. Secondly, he’s incredibly good in those tournaments and has won the Golden Boot in the last two World Cups. Thirdly, he’s only 27.

Mbappe’s goal-scoring rate overall is a little lower than Kane or Lukaku’s but he is making up for any lost time. The Real Madrid forward has got 12 in 13 2026 games. In 2022 and 2023, he broke double digits and since moving centrally, he has only scored more for his club side.

Mbappe has got 67 goals from his 107 games, putting him 78 behind Ronaldo but you would fancy him to get close at the least by the time his career is over.

READ: Will Cristiano Ronaldo get a Portugal farewell match? What we know after retirement announcement

Haaland’s goalscoring ratio makes him a strong contender.

The Norwegian machine has a better-than-a-goal-a-game ratio for his country and, while meaning no disrespect to Norway, he is doing it in not one of the elite nations.

Haaland has got 65 in 59 including five in seven at the most recent World Cup and has age on his side as well with him being 26.

Where Haaland may fall down is that he does not play for one of the top nations and so reaching tournaments and then going deep in them is not a given.

The 2026 World Cup was the first time Norway have appeared in it in 28 years and while you would say they are one of the 48 best teams in the world right now, that could fluctuate more than other nations like France, Germany and Spain.

He’s got a long way to go, but he certainly has the time to get there.

Yamal has 11 goals for Spain so far, putting him a whopping 135 goals behind Ronaldo but Yamal is only 19, meaning he has another 22 years in the game if he were to play to the age of Ronaldo.

Although one of the most impressive attributes about both Ronaldo and Messi is their longevity. Yamal would not be the first young superstar to be off the top perch by the time he was 30.

One other negative for Yamal is he is not an out-and-out goalscorer in the same way Ronaldo in particular was. Yamal got just one goal and four assists in Euro 2024 while he failed to really shine in the 2026 World Cup despite Spain winning it.

If he begins to score more goals, especially for the national team, and if he stays healthy, he could do it but those are two big ifs.

Why Your Houseplant Leaves Are Turning Yellow This November – and the Simple Fix to Reverse the Damage

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

Expert tips for growing fruit, vegetables and herbs at home.

What Your Christmas Cactus Needs in November – Ace These 4 Tasks for Fabulous Holiday Flowers

These 7 Plants Need Pruning Before Winter – November is Your Last Chance to Make the Cut

Why You Should Never Ignore a Spider Plant With Brown Leaf Tips in Fall

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Yellowing leaves this month are usually caused by the #1 seasonal mistake houseplant parents make. Here’s how to save your plant's life before it’s too late.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If your houseplant leaves are turning yellow this November, your plant is sending urgent distress signals that you really need to heed. As days shorten and sunlight becomes less intense in fall, most houseplants use far less moisture as their growth dramatically slows. Continuing your summer watering routine during this shift creates waterlogged soil, quickly leading to root rot – and an avoidable trip to the compost bin. Catch that first yellow leaf early, however, and you can quickly reverse the damage to keep your houseplant happy and healthy.

Failing to adjust a watering routine in line with houseplants’ seasonal needs is the most common mistake we plant parents make, and unfortunately the root rot that results can be fatal. It takes a few weeks of waterlogged soil and rotting roots before the damage manifests with yellowing leaves, which is why this symptom usually shows up in November – and why once you spot a yellowing leaf you need to take swift action.

So, great work getting here to Garden ER where you'll find straightforward solutions to treat this season’s biggest planty problems. Come on in and let’s diagnose and treat your houseplant with a yellowing leaf, and save it from an untimely death with a simple fix.

Take a closer look at the yellow houseplant leaf to see what tone, shape, position and extent of the discoloration is. Different plants show this early symptom of root rot in various ways: a Monstera will likely get a yellow halo on one of its monster-sized leaves while a ZZ plant leaf will turn bright yellow from top to bottom overnight. Peperomia foliage typically fades to a pale yellow while a snake plant leaf takes on a translucent yellow tone at the base. Matching up the symptoms to the plant species is a surefire way to tell if overwatering is the cause.

Match your houseplant to the symptoms and you have your diagnosis.

Large, thin-leafed tropical plants like monstera, calathea, anthurium and clumping philodendron typically show a bright yellow halo around a dark spot from overwatering. This happens because as roots begin to rot in soggy soil, fungal or bacterial pathogens in the potting mix travel up the plant and into the leaf tissue. Dead leaf cells show up as a black or brown area, and the bright ring of yellow around it is where the plant is attempting to stop the infection spreading.

ZZ plants (Zamioculcas zamiifolia), caladiums, and elephant ears (Alocasia and Colocasia) grow from potato-like rhizomes or tubers that store lots of water, so it can take these plants a little longer to show signs of overwatering. When they do, it's dramatic though! If these houseplants receive more water than they can process and rot sets in, nutrients are suddenly cut to a whole stem. The entire stalk and its foliage will quickly drain of color to turn a solid strong yellow, top to bottom.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Prayer plants (Maranta), peperomia, Chinese money plants (Pilea peperomioides), African violets (Saintpaulia), nerve plants (Fittonia) and Boston and maidenhair ferns all have a shallow network of very fine, fragile roots. Suffocating in overwatered soil, these thin roots quickly disintegrate and the leaves lose their lustre then become limp and fade through pale green to a matte yellow. Don’t expect these leaves to be mushy from all that water: because the root damage cuts the water supply off, leaves will become dry and brittle, snapping off immediately when touched or simply dropping from the plant.

Jade plants (Crassula ovata), snake plants, Christmas or Thanksgiving cacti (Schlumbergera), echeverias and aloe vera all store water in their stems or leaves, and excess moisture causes them to turn a translucent, sickly yellow at the base. The area will also feel mushy and this may happen where stems or leaves meet the soil, in which case stems may topple over, but also where leaves attach to stems or leaf segments join together.

Vining plants like pothos (Epipremnum aureum), English ivy (Hedera helix), wax plants (Hoya) and heartleaf philodendron (Philodendron hederaceum) typically show overwatering symptoms on their oldest leaves near the base first. Unable to draw as many nutrients through suffocating roots, the plant strips nutrients from its older, less productive leaves to feed the fresh growth at its tips in the hope of finding a more suitable environment. Stripped of chlorophyll, the base foliage turns an even, uniform yellow while the newer growth at the stem tip remains green.

Overwatered houseplants with long, narrow leaves like spider plants (Chlorophytum comosum) and ponytail palms (Beaucarnea recurvata) typically develop brown foliage tips with a yellow area separating them from the healthy green tissue. This happens because the leaf tips are at the very end of the plant's plumbing system so, when roots are damaged and can't take up water effectively, are the first to run out of moisture. The yellow color below the dead brown leaf tissue is the plant trying to salvage what nutrients it can. The leaves may lose their upright arch and start to droop, too.

If you spot yellowing houseplant leaves, immediately stop watering and let the soil dry out. Slide the pot off and if you see white roots, you’ve caught the problem in time. Pop the plant back into its pot and only water when the top inch of potting mix feels dry. A moisture meter like this from Amazon takes out the guesswork and helps you adjust your routine to the plant's fluctuating needs going forwards, and water houseplants properly.

If there’s a foul, stagnant smell, however faint, and roots look dark, root rot has set in and you’ll need to take action to save your plant. Remove as much soil as possible and use sharp scissors or pruners or to snip off all roots that look dark or feel mushy or slimy. Rinse the roots and wash the pot, then repot your houseplant.

It's vital the pot has a drainage hole so excess moisture can escape (and a saucer to save your surfaces). If you adore a decorative hole-less pot, use it as a cachepot with a holed plastic pot inside. For succulent plants that store water in their leaves or stems, fill your pot with a fast-draining potting mix like Back to the Roots Organic Succulents & Cacti Mix, available from The Home Depot. For everything else, use an indoor potting mix with coconut coir and perlite that evens out moisture levels while allowing good drainage like Miracle-Gro Indoor Potting Mix For All Plants, available from Walmart.

Upgrade your irrigation with a watering can that has a long, gooseneck spout like this indoor can from Walmart for more precise, controlled water delivery.

Every week, the Garden ER series brings you simple solutions to this season's most pressing problems, for happy and healthy plants without any hassle.

Work out what's behind your ailing spider-plant developing brown tips in fall and nurse your houseplant back to health.

Take these proven-to-work steps to make your yard inhospitable to snakes in fall and reduce numbers next year, too.

End rot is a common challenge for winter squash in fall but identify the cause and it's simple to rescue the remaining crop.

A lost leaf or two doesn’t spell disaster for a Christmas cactus in fall but you can't afford to ignore this distress signal.

Chafer grubs have devastated drought-stressed lawns this year but are easily treated if catch this crucial fall window.

Work out what your peace lily needs to recover if spathes develop brown patches or look dry and crispy around the edges.

Emma is an avid gardener and has worked in media for over 25 years. Previously editor of Modern Gardens magazine, she regularly writes for the Royal Horticultural Society. She loves to garden hand-in-hand with nature and her garden is full of bees, butterflies and birds as well as cottage-garden blooms. As a keen natural crafter, her cutting patch and veg bed are increasingly being taken over by plants that can be dried or woven into a crafty project.

« Un vide impossible à combler » : La famille de Jesús de Miguel sort du silence après le drame

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Ce jeudi 5 novembre 2026, la famille de Jesús de Miguel est sortie du silence après la disparition tragique de l'attaquant du CD Tenerife. Devant les médias, son représentant Manuel Hernández a relayé un message poignant de ses proches.

L'attaquant espagnol, âgé de 30 ans, est décédé le mardi 3 novembre 2026 des suites d'un violent choc au thorax survenu en plein match de deuxième division face au FC Andorra. Selon le rapport d'autopsie préliminaire transmis aux autorités judiciaires, le décès est consécutif à un traumatisme thoracique accidentel intervenu lors d'une sortie du gardien adverse Jesús Owono, exclu sur l'action. Une onde de choc pour le football espagnol.

Rassemblés dans la province de Tolède pour affronter cette perte soudaine, les proches du joueur ont tenu à saluer les nombreuses marques d'affection reçues depuis quarante-huit heures. Devant les médias, Manuel Hernández a souligné l'immense gratitude de la famille De Miguel Alameda envers les coéquipiers de l'attaquant, ses amis, les supporters ainsi que les clubs du monde entier qui ont multiplié les hommages. Une solidarité précieuse dans l'épreuve.

Au-delà de ces remerciements, les proches de l'attaquant ont formulé un appel clair au respect de leur douleur. La famille a en effet insisté sur la nécessité de préserver son intimité afin de pouvoir traverser cette terrible épreuve à l'écart de l'agitation médiatique.

Le recueillement s'organise désormais au sein du village natal de Navahermosa. La messe de funérailles de Jesús de Miguel a ainsi été fixée ce jeudi 5 novembre 2026 à 15h00 en l'église paroissiale San Miguel Arcángel. Un dernier hommage solennel pour accompagner le joueur entouré des siens.

La disparition tragique de l'attaquant a suscité une vive émotion jusqu'au sommet du football espagnol. Avant le transfert du corps vers la péninsule, plusieurs personnalités sont venues se recueillir à la chambre funéraire Santa Lastenia à Tenerife. Le président du Real Madrid Florentino Pérez et le directeur des relations institutionnelles Emilio Butragueño ont notamment tenu à faire le déplacement pour manifester leur soutien aux proches et au club insulaire.

Sur le plan sportif, les compétitions passent logiquement au second plan. D'après la presse espagnole, le match de la 13e journée de Liga Hypermotion entre le Granada CF et le CD Tenerife, initialement programmé le samedi 7 novembre 2026 au stade Nuevo Los Cármenes, a été reporté à une date ultérieure. À en croire ces mêmes sources, le CD Tenerife aurait également décrété six jours d'arrêt total de ses activités et prévoirait d'ouvrir les portes du stade Heliodoro Rodríguez López ce samedi pour un hommage populaire.
